### ED07 — Production acceptance and closure

**Objective:** promote only the approved exact capability batch. No inferred channel/device permission from earlier tests.

**Activation order:** verify independently proven Main-admitted operation with Decision routing off, including domain entry/result custody, optional native-child/context and scheduled semantics; this does not make a domain child Run mandatory. Complete G10 on the representative Pi before enabling the P03 fast path. After G9 real-provider readiness and separately approved observational exposure, run bounded non-dispatching shadow; the Main-admitted path retains the sole execution commitment, domains execute their own operations, and shadow results authorize no effects or duplicate replies. Only after the approved measured criteria pass may Oren separately authorize read-only direct cohorts and then relevant mutation cohorts. Keep unavailable/failed gates disabled and label narrower acceptance LIMITED. Cohort bounds never relax Run completion, authority, resource fencing or native delivery requirements.

**Acceptance:** prepare one compact operator handoff for each materially distinct identity/semantics class: preflight, exact human action, expected visible result, deterministic backend assertions, necessary physical observation, cleanup and after-state. Exercise representative user/direct/Main/native-child paths; deterministic background events/restart/native delivery and fresh background Runs only in their approved scope; one verified external-start device warning without fabricated task correlation; cross-path notification deduplication; pending start/expiry/cancel with exact source correlation; preserved Reminder atomicity and Jessica physical scope. Retain evidence of side effects before any retry. Do not provoke a physical fault merely for coverage: use trusted retained event evidence in an approved isolated replay plus a separately approved nonphysical channel canary, and record that evidence limit. Do not use a cleaning-start canary to claim completion.

For enabled unified-entry classes, demonstrate a sufficient Main task with zero additional domain model calls/child Runs, a known event and resolved Automation occurrence with zero model calls, and a genuine domain-reasoning case with an independently authorized fresh Run. Verify the exact native trust mapping for each origin, unchanged exclusive tool ownership, current-grant/cancel enforcement, no duplicate effect/reply and Main's single user-facing interface through Response/native delivery. A direct/domain result must not acquire a mandatory Main wording Run. Reuse deterministic equivalence cases only where identity/semantics proofs permit; unproven bindings remain disabled and named in closure.

**Closure:** all matrix rows required for the exact approved capability batch pass at their stated levels; §1.5 defines bounded Foundation acceptance independently of unrelated unimplemented capabilities. Preserve whole-stage obligations and open gates rather than relabeling them PASS. New exposure and approved deferrals are explicit; independent review PASS and Plan closure current. Oren approves push and PR separately and performs merge. Verify accepted main and required snapshot after sync; runtime snapshots capture intentionally preserved state where required. Do not report deferred D6/steering as implemented.

**Rollback:** disable only failed new exposure; reconcile active/uncertain physical operations, conditional/pending claims and native outbound; restore compatible affected configuration/source and retained records. Files cannot unsend messages or undo cleaning.

### ED-S1 — Deferred active-only steering

Separate future approval, not part of base implementation. Own G4 under native admission/Run lifecycle; retain each input's disposition and original domain operation fencing. Prove active generation, source grant, actual application and cancellation/terminal races with no hidden interruption/follow-up fallback. Stage-specific checkpoint, runtime exposure, negative/production evidence and rollback must be specified before starting. Until then routing must truthfully decline/handle a separate new input rather than claim it steered an active Run.

### ED-C1 — Deferred durable conditional actions

Separate future capability implementation; no new dependency engine/store/scheduling framework. Resolve G7/G8 in existing Reminder/native owners before choosing a concrete record. G5 supplies the exact Jessica source predicate; ED05 is not a prerequisite.

Implement only the Architecture §17.5 one-shot predicate/action shape, with complete target intent, source generation, stable target effect identity, effect-time grant check, native lifecycle/wakeup, validity/time-anchor policy, atomic cancel/claim and reconciliation after ambiguous creation. Registration readback precedes a registered response. Any new persistence surface requires its own explicit decision, even within this stage.

Acceptance includes duplicate/delayed/lost/reordered events, source replacement/failure/cancellation, grant revocation, semantic edit revision, force-run attempting to bypass the predicate, cancellation racing claim, crash after Reminder creation before acknowledgment, restart during every claim phase, expired event, missing completion time and past-due target. Prove exactly one target transaction by stable identity and readback; do not claim exactly-once transport or infer atomicity across HA/OpenClaw/Calendar. Require approved representative source physical evidence and Reminder/channel runtime evidence. Prepare concrete checkpoint/rollback/exposure before implementation; no implicit deletion/compensation of an already created Reminder.

## 11. One-shot TypeScript migration — approved execution design

### 11.1 Objective, scope and evidence baseline

**Approval state: DESIGN APPROVED; TS01 and TS02 MERGED; TS03 independent source/fixture work MERGED, native-dependent integration BLOCKED; complete source-only TS03 execution/publication APPROVED on 2026-10-17.** Oren approved the design and TS01–TS05 sequence, subject to minimal build infrastructure, TypeBox compatibility with existing validated schemas, and TS01 resolution of the supported SDK boundary replacing `api.runtime.tasks` before dependent Jessica implementation. All verification and fail-closed guarantees remain binding. The objective is a clean, strict TypeScript implementation of Benson's active JavaScript business and integration owners, with portable business contracts and minimal OpenClaw adapters, preserving accepted behavior through one controlled production cutover on `2026.10.4`. This is a language/boundary migration within Architecture §§3/6/7.6/8.1/19, not completion of the event-driven roadmap. TS01–TS05 below are bounded migration slices within existing ED responsibilities; they do not replace ED/FC stage acceptance. Oren approved separating the existing OpenClaw upgrade compatibility blocker from independent migration work: Jessica completion custody and its dependent integration remain BLOCKED, while independent TS01 work may complete and subsequent Business Core work requires its normal separate stage approval. All binding, single-cutover and E2E obligations remain prerequisites for their dependent integration/release.

In scope: active routing/admission/completion/Response JavaScript, Jessica domain/core/HA/executor/state/native adapters, Reminder JavaScript contracts/runner/native adapters, shared date formatting, and their affected active tests/checkers. Preserve the correct Reminder Python executable/discovery implementation, its shell entry and existing shell tests. Archived experiments, historical patch fixtures, external operator utilities and unrelated source are not language-migration targets. No new capability, service, store, scheduler, MCP/A2A interface, provider, model call, framework, public tool or OpenClaw core modification is proposed.

Minimum proof of migration success: strict build and schema/type agreement; business tests without loading OpenClaw; native adapter and state compatibility on the pinned target; preservation of all retained contracts and effects; representative approved production/physical acceptance of the changed enabled paths; compatible rollback and final independent PASS. A compile, plugin load or single read canary alone cannot establish that result.

### 11.2 Design decisions and current research

The following migration design choices were approved by Oren on 2026-10-17 within the existing architecture; implementation remains stage-gated. Sources were inspected on 2026-10-17; installed declarations govern target compatibility when rolling documentation is newer.

| Question | Evidence, alternatives and approved design direction |
| --- | --- |
| How to establish one contract for static and runtime data? | Installed OpenClaw uses `typebox:1.3.34`; its `docs/plugins/tool-plugins.md` recommends a declared runtime TypeBox dependency. [TypeBox](https://github.com/sinclairzx81/typebox) derives static types from schema values. Choose schema-first TypeBox in the existing contract owners, infer DTO types, and derive native JSON schemas from those values. Independent handwritten interfaces plus schemas invite drift; [Ajv's TypeScript documentation](https://ajv.js.org/guide/typescript.html) explicitly notes union-completeness limits. A second Zod/JTD contract or a custom schema framework is unnecessary. Pin TypeBox explicitly; do not rely on a transitive SDK import. Semantic authorization/evidence checks remain domain-owned refinements. |
| How strict, and how to execute? | [TypeScript strict](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/strict.html), [indexed access](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/noUncheckedIndexedAccess.html), [optional properties](https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/exactOptionalPropertyTypes.html) and [Node module resolution](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/modules/reference.html) support strict Node ESM compilation. Choose `tsc` ahead-of-time output and the existing Node test runner. [Node type stripping](https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html) does not type-check; loader-only execution is insufficient. No bundler, runtime transpiler, DI container or new test framework. Pin a supported stable compiler and matching Node types after the TS01 declaration check; do not inherit OpenClaw's development compiler automatically. |
| How to obtain genuine harness independence? | Architecture §§3/7.6/19 already separate business and native responsibilities. Choose inward dependencies and narrow injected interfaces under existing owners. Keep one production OpenClaw adapter and deterministic test doubles. A second harness implementation, universal `Harness` object, adapter registry or lifecycle abstraction adds no present proof. Independence means core code and tests require no OpenClaw package, session objects, tools, environment or database paths; it does not replace native infrastructure ownership. |
| Which target-native tool boundary? | Installed `docs/plugins/tool-plugins.md` and [official tool guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/tool-plugins) describe `OpenClawPluginToolContext<2>`, `contextVersion:2` and live `assertInvocationCurrent`. Use the existing plugin IDs/task entries and concrete SDK types. Preserve host authority across awaits, with checks immediately before I/O/disclosure/effect. A serialized requester DTO or type brand is not a grant. [OWASP authorization guidance](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) supports deny-by-default/per-use checks; no duplicated authorization service is needed. |
| Which state API replaces the legacy import? | Installed `dist/plugin-sdk/plugin-state-store-runtime.js` still exports the legacy factory; `dist/plugin-state-store-yKq-W0-T.mjs` marks `createPluginStateSyncKeyedStore` as deprecated compatibility. Installed `PluginRuntime` also marks `state.openSyncKeyedStore` deprecated. Choose `api.runtime.state.openKeyedStore`, native `observe`/`compareAndApply` and atomic `registerIfAbsent`, after target proof. This requires explicit async/currentness and conflict handling but avoids carrying the legacy blocking callback into the clean rewrite. Keeping synchronous callbacks is the smaller mechanical conversion, not the proposed long-term binding. Reject unlocked read/modify/write or a custom database. Prove namespace/key/data and atomic-transition parity in TS01/TS04. |
| Can the existing Jessica completion wiring be typed unchanged? | `J/dist/index.js` supplies `api.runtime.tasks`; installed `PluginRuntime` declarations do not expose it. Task/transcript completion custody therefore needs an exact supported replacement proof, not an ambient declaration or cast. Existing session/transcript and subagent APIs are candidate evidence sources, not equivalent authority by name. G1(b)/G2 and retained last-tool equality remain binding. If no supported path preserves the enabled obligation, stop that migration dependency before cutover; no core shim, privileged RPC or weaker completion check. |
| Should Python/scheduling be rewritten? | `agents/reminder-service/libexec/reminder-service-core` owns atomic Reminder/Calendar behavior and uses native `automations` CLI commands; `M/dist/runner.js` calls its fixed executable with argv, without a shell. KEEP both semantics and Python. A typed whole-transaction port and the existing process adapter isolate that dependency from the TypeScript core. This preserves an explicitly OpenClaw-backed implementation; it does not claim Python is harness-neutral or port Reminder mechanics into another owner. |

Installed anchors: `dist/agent-harness-runtime-DZjstJNb.d.ts` contains tool context/factory declarations at lines 11902–11981, native state observation/CAS contracts at 8105–8192 and runtime state factories at 32419–32427. The async observation/CAS members are optional in the public store type; TS01 must prove their concrete availability, then the adapter fails closed at initialization if absent. `observe` uses writable admission and may create state, so its isolated proof must never point at production. `json-schema-runtime` and the legacy state subpath lack their own declaration files; use the canonical schema library and typed runtime factories instead of ambient declarations or casts. Preserve `definePluginEntry` plus v2 factories: the convenience `defineToolPlugin` factory context is not automatically equivalent to v2 authority.

Upstream provenance: [2026.10.4 release](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/releases/tag/v2026.10.4), [SDK overview](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-overview) and [state guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-runtime/state-and-system). Rolling documentation can name APIs newer than this release; examples such as keyed-store V2 or async session helpers must not be selected without target exports/types. Public declarations, allowed third-party provenance and actual invocation are separate checks. Installed `dist/server-plugins-C1SSiMcB.mjs` restricts `api.runtime.gateway.request` to bundled/trusted-official provenance; it is not a Benson fallback. An authenticated HTTP/RPC dispatch contract would introduce a separate registration/security surface and is outside this proposal. Existing core drift inventory remains §8; nothing here reapplies or deletes its artifacts.

### 11.3 Canonical source layout and dispositions

Use the existing owners, not a second top-level business platform. `R`, `J` and `M` retain §3's meanings. The paths below define the approved source organization; execution approvals and current slice status are owned by §§11.6/11.10–11.12.

| Existing source → planned source | Disposition and responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `R/{envelope,request-control,completion-control,response-control}.mjs` → `R/core/*.ts` | REPLACE with small typed modules in the same routing owner. Keep envelope as the common schema export owner; split private version codecs only when needed for readability. Request handles, source authority, no-Run outcomes, actual Run settlement and public DomainResultV2 stay distinct. Move native context acquisition into `R/adapters/openclaw/*.ts`; no SDK import in core. |
| `R/{classification,policy,evaluation,telemetry,presentation}.mjs`, `rubric.json` | REPAIR/REPLACE active logic in the same owner. Preserve budgets/corpus and disabled activation state. Move domain-specific wording into its domain owner exactly once; generic Response validates/prepares the result. Remove obsolete engine policy only after consumer proof; do not enable Decision/Response models. |
| `agents/jessica-vacuum/lib/*.mjs` → corresponding `.ts`; `J/dist/{executor,operation-state}.js` → that existing domain `lib` | REPLACE JavaScript with contract-first domain code; relocate business transitions into Jessica's existing core owner. Keep registry/policy JSON as the reviewed data owners. HA transport/credential loading remains in domain `lib/adapters/`; pure projections/predicates are separate from I/O. Preserve dispatch claims, resource fences, uncertain barriers and verifier meaning. |
| `J/dist/{index,tool,execute-tool,identity,completion}.js` → `J/src/*.ts` | REPLACE host wiring with thin typed registration, trusted-context conversion, native state/session access and serialization. Domain authorization, plans and effect verification remain in Jessica core. Keep plugin ID and public tool names/schemas except separately reviewed compatible metadata. No `runtime.tasks` compatibility shim. |
| `M/dist/{contracts,runner,index}.js` → domain portions in `agents/reminder-service/lib/*.ts`, host/process portions in `M/src/*.ts` | REPLACE JavaScript; retain canonical Python transaction/discovery and roster/link metadata. The logical contract owner is unchanged. Native route/transcript parsing stays in the adapter; pure Reminder request/result validation and wording stay domain-local. Do not duplicate Python schedule/permission/compensation policy. |
| `shared/benson-datetime.mjs` → `.ts` | REPLACE language only; preserve timezone/DST and display semantics. No new scheduling owner. |
| Existing domain/routing/plugin tests, fixtures and canary checkers | REPAIR affected imports/contracts; migrate active JS checks to strict TS under their current test owners. Preserve actual production tool/Code Mode shapes and failure fixtures. Consolidate proven equivalent checker mechanics only within the existing generic checker owner; no historical canary replay. |
| Existing plugin `package.json`/`openclaw.plugin.json`; planned root private `package.json`, lockfile and `tsconfig.json` | EXTEND existing packaging and add the minimum shared build/typecheck configuration. These design-approved build surfaces require TS01 implementation approval and do not change runtime registration or permissions. Keep plugin entry `dist/index.js`; all distributed JavaScript is generated, never hand-maintained alongside TS. |
| Proven dead engine implementations/exports and spent adapters | REMOVE only after reference/import/loaded-consumer audit and replacement acceptance. Strict legacy codecs remain when records/callers require them, with named consumers and exit conditions. P05/S09 recovery evidence and §8 core artifacts are preserved; historical evidence is not an active alternative implementation. |

Dependency direction: native composition roots → typed adapters/domain entry → business contracts and policy. Business modules may use ordinary Node primitives and the chosen schema library; they may not import `openclaw`, SDK types, native database/session implementations, plugin entrypoints or global runtime configuration. Domain/device/recipient authorization is not moved into generic Ports. Unknown SDK/file/HA/subprocess/model data enters as `unknown` and is decoded before business use.

Compilation uses one root source tree and emits only selected TS inputs into a candidate tree under the existing ignored `output/` owner. Assembly maps the two emitted plugin `src/` trees to their existing `dist/` locations and preserves the relative layout of shared/domain outputs; SDK/dependency imports remain external. A deterministic allowlist/digest records every runtime artifact, copied JSON and consumer. Prove the assembled package's complete dependency closure, path resolution and native capture/loading before publication. Source tests run against emitted candidates; artifact smoke tests exercise the assembled entrypoints. No worktree, copied checkout, live source watcher, second runtime or dual execution path is required.

Until TS05, current production entrypoints and their dependency bytes stay unchanged. Candidate TS is the sole new authored implementation; old deployed JavaScript is frozen rollback/runtime material, not a second evolving source. Published output must match the reviewed TS/compiler/lockfile digest. After accepted cutover, remove superseded active JavaScript only with dependency proof and retained rollback; keep generated runtime output distinguishable from authored source.

### 11.4 Typed contracts and minimal Ports

Compiler contract: `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `noImplicitReturns`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `useUnknownInCatchVariables`, `verbatimModuleSyntax` and `noEmitOnError`; explicit Node ESM target/module resolution and supported Node library types. TypeScript production sources use no explicit/implicit `any`, blanket `@ts-ignore`, `as unknown as`, unvalidated non-null assertions or ambient declarations that invent SDK support. `as const`, `satisfies` and proof-local branded constructors are appropriate; an unavoidable foreign declaration defect requires an isolated documented proof and approval, not relaxation of the project boundary. Do not use `skipLibCheck` to hide the target SDK incompatibility.

Schema values own transport/record structure; inferred types do not repeat it. Preserve closed objects, collection/string/byte/depth bounds, safe numbers, exact optional/null distinctions and discriminated version/origin/operation/status/effect variants. Use exhaustive `never` checks for finite variants. Domain refinements enforce correlated identities, scope, current policy, source freshness and effect/reconciliation relationships after structural decoding. Validation never coerces, drops unknown fields, inserts defaults or upgrades a version silently. Registry-driven tool subsets are projections of the same domain contract plus approved capability data, not another allowlist.

Use interfaces for effects and generics only where the same operation is genuinely shared. Prefer functions/composition and structural polymorphism over a base domain class or service locator. The following private interfaces describe consumed capabilities, not new public tools, authorities, stores or implementations:

| Port / owner | Minimum typed behavior and native binding obligation |
| --- | --- |
| `InvocationAuthority` / existing admission and domain identity | Host adapter supplies validated source-qualified context plus `assertCurrent(): void` and cancellation. The native handle stays nonserializable and invocation-scoped. Business policy still checks subject/action/target; v2 reads do not acquire durable mutation grants. Effects additionally require existing original input/operation identity and current delegated authority. |
| `ConversationEvidence` / existing request-control custody | Bounded canonical reads at the immutable admission cutoff, exact sender/conversation binding and explicit partial/unavailable results. Preserve §4.4 tiers; never expose a raw SDK session or substitute concatenated arbitrary history for trusted turns. |
| `AtomicKeyedState<Records>` / Jessica operation owner | Typed asynchronous lookup, observation/compare-and-apply and atomic `registerIfAbsent` over the existing closed key families; decode stored values as versioned `unknown`. Pure domain transitions prepare data from an observation; native comparison commits atomically. Preserve capacity/durability and return explicit applied/unchanged/conflict. An opaque comparison token is not authority: bind the native currentness guard using `withCurrent`, recheck across awaits and keep the final pre-effect fence. On conflict, return a typed conflict/reconcile result; no automatic mutation retry. Ambiguous commit requires readback/reconciliation. Separate keys are not one transaction; preserve/reconcile current index/history ordering. No callback emulation, process-local lock or synchronous compatibility fallback. |
| `JessicaDevice` / existing HA read/control owner | Bounded read projections and allowlisted dispatch of an authorized immutable domain plan; explicit no-effect/possible-effect/verified evidence. Carry currentness/fence checks to the existing final pre-POST point. No generic entity/service/command API reaches the core caller. |
| `ReminderTransaction` / existing Reminder owner | Typed operation-specific request → versioned validated result of the preserved executable. The adapter owns fixed argv, bounded output/deadline/cancellation and safe errors. Abort/timeout after possible mutation is uncertainty requiring the existing reconciliation path, never proof of no effect or a fresh create retry. |
| Existing Run-evidence and native-response dependencies / completion and Response owners | Type only the evidence, eligible handoff and receipt operations actually consumed by migrated code. SDK Runs/records remain adapter-private. Retain strict last-tool/caller/result equality and complete DomainResultV2 serialization. G2/G3 capabilities not already bound remain disabled; do not implement speculative `RunManager`, delivery store or fake-success adapter. |
| Clock and diagnostic callbacks / consuming existing owner | Explicit clock and bounded redacted observation inputs for deterministic tests; retain existing time semantics and native diagnostic ownership. No time service, timer scheduler or parallel telemetry store. |

The common DTO remains `DomainResultV2`, derived from validated read no-Run v2/v3 or an actual bound completion v5/v6, each under its own strict source/custody profile. `executionMode` comes from the trusted path, never from model input. Preserve separate handling/business/quality/effect states, prepared content and uncertainty. No-Run rejection/reasoning-required cannot fabricate a Run or activate one. Identical validated values must survive both direct native and Code Mode carriers; `outputSchema` is supplementary, not authorization or proof of complete model-visible content.

Portability acceptance compiles and executes the core/domain test graph with only Node and declared schema dependencies, no OpenClaw resolver/runtime. An import-graph assertion in the existing source-contract test owner rejects transitive SDK/native imports from core. Test doubles implement the same private Ports and assert effect traces; they prove business independence, not native authority. A second production harness is neither built nor registered.

### 11.5 Compatibility and state contract

This migration does not bump a public/durable version just because the source language changes. It retains the §4.1 compatibility obligations and the actual producer/consumer inventory. TS01 must enumerate every active writer, reader, native entry, CLI/checker, loaded dependency and retained record version; no inferred absence from a failed inventory query.

| Surface | Required migration behavior / retirement condition |
| --- | --- |
| Request/control v1; source admission v1; same-invocation admission/no-Run v2; no-Run v1 | Preserve exact accepted variants and effect-aware identity rules. Opaque live handles cannot be decoded from stored JSON or retained across restart. Unknown source/version rejects before read/disclosure/dispatch. |
| Completion v3/v4/v5 and DomainResultV2/Response v2 | Preserve identified legacy callers/readers until a proven supported successor replaces them. No silent default switch to v5, reinterpretation of removed workflow fields or loss of vendor saved-program IDs. Inventory active legacy producers explicitly; unsupported completion custody blocks their replacement, not their validation requirement. Retire writers/readers only after consumer and retention proof. |
| Jessica `jessica-operations-v1`, device record v2, request/operation/under-test records | Preserve namespace, physical backing owner, keys, encoding, capacity, generations, revisions, requestEpoch, idempotency keys, digests and known/possible effects. Preserve fail-closed release; a terminal Run/result is not released hardware. Cross-Run original identity remains its existing ED02 gate; no language-only key change. |
| Reminder Python protocol, native jobs and Calendar links | Preserve executable protocol and Python-owned semantics, creator/recipient visibility, original-turn citations, timezone/DST, atomic staging/readback/enablement/compensation and native IDs/revisions. TS decoding validates its existing boundary; it does not create competing Python business rules or a copied job registry. |
| Native sessions, Automation definitions/grants/history and frozen outbound | No migration-owned direct SQL, native-record rewrite, queue cleanup or payload regeneration. Preserve unresolved native probe/delivery history. Native package/database downgrade is outside this cutover. |

Prefer no business-data transformation. If the supported store API changes physical addressing, encoding, transaction semantics or required record format, TS01 must produce a reviewed mapping, dry-run count/hash/integrity proof, writer fence and forward/rollback compatibility before any state write. That is a separate significant delta requiring approval, not an implicit TypeScript step. Unknown/corrupt records retain recoverable evidence and block affected writes; never create an empty replacement store. No old/new dual writers or dual sends.

### 11.6 Ordered execution slices and gates

TS01's independent foundation is accepted and merged in PR #45 (`e720fbe6b88070048a6f2eca6bc0a44e965b7366`); TS02 is accepted and merged in PR #46 (`c8d35e66124973c23528bdf67d9a676f6dc95df7`). TS03's original independent source/fixture acceptance in PR #47 remains dated evidence; subsequent native and concrete-adapter work reached final cumulative PASS under §11.43 and merged in PR #53. This accepts TS03 at source/concrete-adapter and isolated-native levels only. Oren authorized starting TS04 on 2026-11-01; its opening/approved registration are in §11.44 and the approved runtime-guidance alignment is in §11.45 and its historical preserved-capability extension is in §11.46. The final operator decision and bounded current acceptance are in §11.55. TS05's separately bounded §11.55 two-read release is Production E2E PASS under §11.64; final publication/merge/snapshot remain open. Earlier failed activations retain their dated limits in §11.63. Each slice uses a primary-checkout branch/PR and its own significant-stage approval under root AGENTS.md; source-only merges must not alter active runtime dependencies. One coherent migration can have several reviewed source slices while retaining exactly one production cutover. No whole ED/FC stage becomes PASS through a TS slice.

| Slice / existing ED responsibility | Change and minimum proof | Checkpoint, dependency and stop condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **TS01 — independent foundation and target-binding inventory** / ED01, ED02, ED06 | Inventory exact producers/consumers, source→output layout, runtime import closure, versions and enabled behavior. Prove the independent target SDK/state surfaces and record provenance. Keep the supported replacement for `api.runtime.tasks`, preserving Jessica completion/caller custody, as a separately BLOCKED dependency of the affected completion adapter/integration, not of unrelated TS01 or Business Core work. Define the one schema source per owner and prove TypeBox compatibility with existing validated schemas; pin compiler/Node types/TypeBox/target SDK and minimal root build configuration. Freeze the independent contract/build/state evidence and identify unresolved bindings explicitly; finalize each affected integration/exposure only after its binding proof. | Source checkpoint plus fixture-only state. Oren approved package installation/build tools, contract/schema compatibility and isolated native-state probes. No production touch. An unresolved binding blocks only dependent implementation/integration; continue independent approved proof work. STOP the affected surface if preserved obligations require private/unsupported API, authority is missing, or storage mapping needs an unapproved migration. Record the gap rather than inventing a port implementation. |
| **TS02 — common business contracts and controllers** / ED02, ED03 | Implement schema-first contracts, strict DTOs/Ports, generic admission/response/completion validation and shared time code. Preserve budgets and actual payload fixtures. Move only proven domain wording to its owner. Run `npm run typecheck`, focused owner tests and core import/portability checks; verify bounds, unknown versions, malformed/forged correlation, effect-preserving failures and legacy read matrix. | Git plus affected uncommitted source checkpoint; no runtime/state checkpoint needed. Requires separate TS02 approval and accepted independent TS01 contract/build evidence. Generic completion validation may migrate as pure Business Core; unproven native custody acquisition remains disabled and is not fabricated by test doubles. Candidate output stays outside runtime paths. STOP on unsafe casts, weaker validation, SDK leakage, duplicate contracts or a changed accepted semantic needing approval. Rollback is source-only. |
| **TS03 — domain cores and concrete adapters** / ED02, ED03, ED06 | Rewrite Jessica core/executor/state and both native plugins; isolate HA/process/native adapters. Preserve Python and domain registries/policy. Bind currentness and native state with actual target SDK types. Run focused domain/state/adapter regressions first, then the affected suites once; use fake HA/process effects plus the real isolated native state adapter for races/restart. Verify original mutation identity and cleanup/uncertainty behavior, without expanding capability scope. | Source checkpoint and disposable fixture records only. Requires separate TS03 approval, TS02 and each surface's relevant TS01 proof. Independent domain-core work need not wait for Jessica completion custody; the affected completion adapter and dependent integration remain BLOCKED until the supported binding is proven. No registration/reload or production effects. STOP on completion/state authority gaps, weaker fences, incompatible existing records or effect trace differences outside approved corrections. Rollback is source/fixture-only. |
| **TS04 — assembled release and recovery rehearsal** / ED06 | Build one candidate artifact closure, validate existing manifests/tool schemas and test assembled entries against exactly `2026.10.4`. Rehearse state/read compatibility, stale-writer exclusion and source rollback on isolated fixtures. Reuse affected test evidence; add exact carrier/publication cases not already proven. Audit active legacy consumers and removal eligibility; record artifact/source/lockfile digests, resource budget and final activation/rollback commands. Obtain fresh independent PASS on the complete final migration diff. | Checkpoint accepted source, candidate digests and rehearsal evidence; protect existing runtime. Depends on TS03 PASS and supported native loading/custody proof. STOP for unknown loaded dependencies, schema/compiler disagreement, insufficient disk/RAM headroom, unsafe recovery or an unproven preserved capability. No production shadow, background watcher or hidden side effects. |
| **TS05 — single controlled cutover and acceptance** / ED06, ED07 | After exact action-time exposure approval, quiesce affected native admissions/writers through supported owners, reconcile in-flight work, checkpoint affected preserved state, publish the one reviewed artifact/config batch, apply the minimum supported lifecycle transition and attest loaded bytes/dependencies/policy. Run the §11.7 separately approved human E2E matrix; preserve before/after/cleanup evidence. Retire eligible old active code only after replacement acceptance and retained recovery. | Depends on TS04 PASS, current preflight, approved release scope, restorable compatible checkpoint and all applicable native/physical prerequisites. New admissions stay gated on failure; reconcile before any retry. No implicit canary/reload retry. Source/config rollback is §11.8; no native downgrade or effect replay. Final PASS, operator merge and accepted main sync precede final snapshot as required. |

TS01 established root `npm run typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) and `npm run build` (selected candidate emission); affected Node tests exercise emitted candidates as recorded in each slice's evidence. Final artifact assembly remains TS04 work. Preserve `node --test` and existing Python/shell test owners. TS01 records the exact installed native validation/publication syntax rather than assuming historical flags still apply. TS01 installation/build and isolated fixtures are approved; runtime publication and every subsequent stage remain separately gated.

The full migration target must not be silently reduced to work around missing completion/state support. Oren explicitly approved the separately bounded two-read release on 2026-11-03 (§11.55), with temporary unavailability of all legacy Benson capabilities accepted and existing data retained. TS04/TS05 acceptance for this release follows that exact scope; remaining full-migration/Phase 1 obligations stay open. No unrelated ED04/ED05/FC/D6 capability becomes a prerequisite merely because it is in the long-term architecture. G2 applies to actual migrated domain Run obligations; Main Response interception remains a separate G3 obligation; the read-only exception does not exempt a new reasoning/mutation path. Existing Reminder native scheduling evidence is retained; current active scheduled effects still require the minimal cutover safety check.

### 11.7 Validation and E2E acceptance matrix

Use the smallest affected proof set, preserving earlier evidence only when its relevant code/native assumptions remain applicable. Tests must assert externally meaningful outcomes/effect traces and recovery invariants, not mirror TypeScript declarations. Each row below maps to existing acceptance owners; failure blocks its dependent cutover scope. For the approved §11.55 release, migrated paths are Jessica status and Reminder full-list only. Mutation, physical and Domain Run rows retain their original obligations for a future migration; legacy execution/Completion/session compatibility is explicitly excluded from this initial release (§11.55), without deleting data or completing their remaining migration obligations. They are not implementation or canary prerequisites here.

| Proof / existing acceptance | Required evidence and expected result |
| --- | --- |
| Types, schemas and portability / A02/A05/A14 | Clean strict build; schema-inferred types, exhaustive variants, boundary `unknown`; invalid/missing/additional/out-of-range/unknown-version payloads reject. Real legacy/DomainResult/Code Mode fixtures retain bounds, content, effects and gaps. Core tests pass without OpenClaw; no forbidden transitive imports. Compiled artifact schemas equal the canonical schema projections. |
| Authority and native custody / A03/A20/A22 | Positive owner/requester and essential foreign/missing/forged/stale/revoked/currentness negatives at the actual target boundary. Await/cancel before disclosure or dispatch cannot reuse authority. Preserve §1.5.1's limited G11 deferral without extending it to writes. Domain semantics/verification remain inside the domain; structured task selection adds zero domain model calls. |
| State and effects / A13/A14 | Actual native adapter fixture races across processes, duplicate same input versus different deliberate input, conflicting digest, corrupt/unknown record, capacity failure, crash around claim/POST/index writes, restart and stale generation. At most one dispatch per claimed step; uncertain effects retain the barrier. Native factory replacement must find the same existing records and preserve fail-closed release. |
| Main read handoff / A07/A13/A22 | After cutover and individual canary approval, Oren sends one Jessica status request and one full Reminder-list request. Verify authentic Main/native task invocation, complete model-visible DomainResultV2 including prepared content, source/observation time and complete reminder/event distinctions, zero domain Runs/mutation/fallback and one reply each. Compare essential meaning, not verbatim prose. Use both direct and Code Mode isolated carrier tests; claim production acceptance only for the carrier actually observed. |
| Jessica preserved physical classes / A13 and FC acceptance | Before physical activation, prove fresh map/policy/currentness and supported original requester/Run/result custody. After separate action-time approval, use representative single-room clean/pause, the exact accepted unordered pair clean/pause, guest-bathroom dock and guest-bathroom strong-setting behavior where the migrated dispatch/verifier boundary changed. Combine compatible observations only with an approved preflight and independently proven target/setting identity; do not clean all 13 rooms. Preserve historical evidence for demonstrably unchanged subpaths. No home/resume/stop/order/repeats/completion expansion. |
| Reminder preserved transaction / A13 | Keep Python source unchanged and run affected existing protocol/provenance/atomicity/DST/Calendar fixtures once. Because the native/process adapter changes, prepare one separately approved representative reversible Reminder+Calendar transaction with multiple schedule/recipient cases covered deterministically; verify native job/link/readback, original authority and one authorized native occurrence if its payload path changed. Cleanup only the exact test records via existing owners, with zero unintended future jobs. A list-only canary cannot accept changed mutation transport. No message or Calendar mutation without its exact approval. |
| Domain Run completion / A05/A06/A14 | Every migrated enabled domain Run/caller path preserves exact authoritative last-tool equality, independent quality/business result and possible effects on malformed/error/cancel/timeout/terminal output. Exercise the real target binding in isolation and representative approved channel execution. Do not claim global G2 PASS from a natural-final hook, or use a fabricated v5 report to avoid an unavailable legacy binding. |

Record latency, model calls and available token/cost observations without inventing telemetry or treating one sample as a benchmark. |

For every human interaction prepare preflight, one exact prompt/action, expected visible meaning, backend assertions, required physical observation, cleanup and final safe state in one handoff. Prefer Oren's real interaction followed by deterministic verification. A failed preflight causes no interaction or POST. Jessica movement and Reminder mutation canaries remain separate authority/semantics classes; read acceptance cannot substitute for either.

TS acceptance excludes new HA ingress/notifications, pending cleaning, active steering/Progress, new Decision routing, P03 fast-path activation, Jessica schedules, conditional actions and unaccepted FC functionality. Their existing ED/FC gates remain unchanged and disabled; this is preservation of existing scope, not a new Phase 2 deferral. If Oren later approves an additional deferral, update `PHASE2_BACKLOG.md` once with its canonical references.

### 11.8 Controlled cutover, rollback and retention

Before TS05 prepare exact approved paths/config fields, artifact digests and native lifecycle commands. Re-read only facts that can change the next action: current installed/loaded target, source/policy parity, relevant active Runs/operations, uncertain claims, native scheduled work/deliveries and available disk/RAM. Startup/restore can process pending work; prove it is safe without deleting records. The earlier 2026.10.4 upgrade/startup/canary approvals are consumed and confer no new exposure.

Checkpoint only the affected nonsecret recoverable state through existing supported owners, with writer coordination, hashes/modes and a verified restore procedure. Retain the current verified native base-plus-delta recovery set identified in ED06 and unresolved-operation history; it is not permission to restore old database bytes over newer history. Never request, print, copy into planning artifacts or manually edit authentication/secret stores. Existing supported recovery protections remain intact.

One cutover means one reviewed source/capability batch and normally one native publication/reload operation; if the supported lifecycle requires a Gateway restart, name that exact action for approval before it is performed. Do not progressively expose partial TS domains during development. Quiescence and re-enable sequencing must fence both old and candidate writers without resetting native jobs, device ownership or idempotency. Jessica stays disabled until its target compatibility and required exposure conditions are established.

On failure: stop new affected admission/dispatch through the approved native boundary; inspect applied generation and durable effects before retry; restore only compatible source/registration/config from the verified checkpoint; verify loaded identity and preserved records. Retain the accepted, verified native runtime and compatible database formats identified in §11.64. The earlier OpenClaw `2026.10.4` baseline is historical, not an active recovery instruction. Historical Jessica or Reminder source is a rollback candidate only after compatibility with the accepted native version and any later state is proven; otherwise leave the affected exposure disabled and preserve its state. No down-conversion, clearing uncertainty, automatic device compensation, message resend or frozen-payload regeneration.

No new database schema is intended; if one is approved later, old writers must stay fenced until the explicit reverse-compatibility condition holds. Files cannot undo a cleaning, Calendar event, Reminder occurrence or sent message. Reconcile these through their existing owners and exact approved cleanup actions.

After observed acceptance, independently review the final applicable diff and closure; each push remains separately approved, Oren alone merges, then verify accepted main and the required fresh `bensonsnap`. Runtime/state snapshot timing follows the actual accepted/preserved-state gate, not a preparation milestone. Remove candidate/checkpoint artifacts only after proving the last known-good recovery and unresolved-operation evidence remain sufficient; significant cleanup requires approval.

Architecture, Jessica capability plan, engineering instructions, implementation, runtime configuration and unrelated `main/DREAMS.md` remain unchanged.

Run focused documentation assertions and `git diff --check`, then the configured fresh isolated read-only reviewer against the full merge-base-to-working-tree diff. Store its verdict and exact reviewed Plan hash with this stage's validation evidence; do not substitute Builder inspection. Product tests, installation, live runtime validation, snapshots and E2E are inapplicable to this planning-only stage and are not performed.

**Operator decision and publication scope — 2026-10-17:** Oren approved the private source separation and Ports, schema-first TypeBox/strict compiler/build design, no-business-data-conversion compatibility strategy, and TS01–TS05 sequence with one production cutover. Keep build infrastructure minimal and TypeBox contracts compatible with existing validated schemas. TS01 records the unresolved supported SDK boundary replacing `api.runtime.tasks`; it must be resolved before the affected Jessica completion adapter and dependent integration proceed, preserving verification and fail-closed guarantees. Independent foundation and Business Core work may proceed through their own approvals. This approval covers the design only. TS01's bounded source/build/isolated proof work and every later stage require their own exact approval; no implementation or runtime publication/change is authorized.

Oren separately directed publication of this reviewed Plan through the canonical engineering workflow. ### 11.15 TS03 isolated native correlation fixture — 2026-10-22

**Authority and scope.** Oren approved bounded fixture implementation and execution of §11.14's next nonphysical proof after verifying isolation and no external effects. Primary branch `migration/ts03-native-correlation-proof` starts at accepted `47581e567efb53581ed52de1af5e1715dda351ab`, retaining the prior local §11.14 publication-status correction. No commit, push, PR, merge, production exposure, device or user-channel action is authorized.

**Isolation and target.** The fixture uses unmodified public `api.runtime.subagent.run/waitForRun`, `before_tool_call` and version 2 tool factories on OpenClaw `2026.10.4`, Node `24.21.0`. Linux namespaces expose only loopback with no external routes, no host home/auth stores, a cleared environment, read-only dependencies/emitted candidates and disposable `/tmp`. A loopback-only deterministic HTTP provider supplies synthetic responses; the only exposed tool is a disposable variant of the existing `jessica_read`, returning a synthetic status validated by the unchanged domain owner. It grants no production requester authority. No real provider, HA transport, channel or device is reachable. The first stock-launcher attempt found a pending package lifecycle marker and failed on the read-only mount. A verified invisible tmpfs SDK overlay then let the stock launcher perform its ordinary package lifecycle; the installed SDK remains the read-only lower layer, with its original marker retained. No guard bypass, private import, package upgrade, core edit, persistent service or second checkout is introduced.

**Native evidence and choice.** Current [upstream implementation](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/src/gateway/server-plugin-subagent-runtime.ts), its [pinned version](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/v2026.10.4/src/gateway/server-plugin-subagent-runtime.ts), official [background-work documentation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-runtime/background-work) and installed public declarations were inspected. Prefer the pinned public Gateway runtime and hooks over private harness imports or an SDK upgrade. The direct tool carrier gives the smallest observable mapping; automatic tool search/Code Mode introduces another unproven carrier and is disabled only in the disposable fixture. No upstream capability or rolling documentation is treated as installed runtime proof.

**Observed validation and limits.** Strict `npm run build` PASS. The focused emitted test PASS (one test, two concurrent native Runs, two domain-validated tool results, four local provider requests, both native terminal observations `ok`). ### 11.18 TS03 fail-closed boundary and recoverable-response proof — 2026-10-25

**Approved scope and recovery.** Oren authorized making missing outbound requirements explicit and investigating/implementing supported isolated G3 proofs, preserving existing owners. Section 10 explicitly requires every accepted reply-eligible request to reach an authorized response or truthful recoverable failure; suppression or completed ingress alone is insufficient. Recovery preserves uncertainty and reconciles before retry. These clarify the existing owners, not a new grant, DTO, store, transport or acceptance relaxation.

**Supported alternatives and limits.** Installed `2026.10.4` public `reply-runtime` provides `beforeDeliver`, `appendBeforeDeliver`, per-stage deadlines and settled receipts. Its [pinned dispatcher](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/v2026.10.4/src/auto-reply/reply/reply-dispatcher.ts) catches pre-handoff failure without calling `deliver`. This is useful local enforcement, not an immutable final boundary: later native stages can change the payload and the callback is not reconstructed for recovery. The [official channel outbound API](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-channel-outbound) and installed `ChannelMessageAdapterShape` provide `send.lifecycle.beforeSendAttempt` in the channel adapter owner, not global application middleware; its context lacks proven Response authority and the installed call has no deadline. Public `durableFinal.admitDeferredDelivery` checks live/recovery admission synchronously, but receives only config/channel/destination/account/phase, not the frozen payload or eligibility binding. It cannot establish per-response authorization. Last-handoff fencing exists internally; no `@internal` handle is used. Native durable sends preserve queue custody and expose explicit outcomes, but do not supply the missing Response authority bridge. Native ingress adoption completes before reply settlement; a local failed receipt or ingress tombstone is not a durable recoverable reply outcome. Do not repurpose ingress failure state after adoption or add an outcome ledger.

The isolated Main test PASS (one test, zero failures), retaining the prior four takeover cases, six outbound cases and ordinary native Main/controller positive path. Namespace preflight confirms zero external interfaces/routes and hidden host home; no channel transport exists. Current raw report/logs and final source/emitted hashes are retained in the checkpoint. Unchanged correlation/isolation code retains dated evidence; its separate suite is not claimed rerun.

**Acceptance boundary.** This accepts only canonical clarification and isolated native primitive characterization, subject to fresh independent review of the stable cumulative diff. It does not implement or accept universal outbound enforcement or durable no-silent-failure handling. Installed `agent-runner.runtime` omits `onAgentRunTerminalOutcome` for cancelled execution and has late failure paths after a completed observation. `agent_end` is per-attempt/fire-and-forget, not a settled delivery barrier; no such event was observed by these terminal fixtures' settled snapshots. ### 11.20 TS03/G3 conclusive native extension recommendation — 2026-10-25

**Historical universal-scope recommendation, superseded for Phase 1 by Oren on 2026-10-26 (§11.21).** No core-extension prototype or source-copy exception is approved or required by the bounded Phase 1 direction. Retain the missing-boundary evidence for Phase 2 only.

**Decision status and scope.** Oren requested a concrete smallest reliable solution using the existing evidence, exhausting remaining public integrations. One fresh read-only Astra xhigh investigator examined the unresolved native seams. The recommendation below is a proposal requiring Oren's explicit core/public-surface/persistence approval, not an accepted architectural change or permission to implement it. No stage/dependency/acceptance is changed. This slice extends only the existing Main fixture and this evidence closure. Architecture, production/core/configuration and unrelated work remain unchanged.

**Additional observed proof.** Public `dispatchInboundMessage.onSettled` runs exactly once for each of the four existing actual native terminal cases, including causal abort. It supplies no terminal result or durable eligibility. A separate public `settleReplyDispatcher` test enqueues a payload from `onSettled`: the returned receipt records zero deliveries, while a later idle receipt records one. Cleanup can observe settlement but is not a final response barrier; adding a fallback there does not make the original receipt cover it. Strict build and isolated Main test PASS; existing error/outcome/candidate accounting assertions remain mandatory. The Main fixture retains and reruns its existing local/outbound cases; unchanged separate correlation evidence is reused at mapping-only level and is not represented as rerun. The public Main `onAgentRunStart` call supplies no `ReplyDispatchRun` completion offer; the inspected ACP offer returns an empty result. Neither that declaration nor an execution identity token is reply eligibility.

**Alternatives exhausted for the pinned target.** Existing global hooks fail open; a configured local dispatcher gate is not final or recovery-wide. `createChannelReplyPipeline.transformReplyPayload` has no trusted route/authority context. Channel `message.send.lifecycle.beforeSendAttempt` additionally misses formatted and legacy `outbound` sends: installed `deliver-prepare` routes those directly through `dispatchToAdapter`. Deferred admission lacks payload; session send policies lack per-response authority. Public durable-message helpers retain transport ownership but do not supply the missing grant or supported stable application-intent adoption: the tempting delivery-intent/completion/handoff fields are explicitly `@internal`. No private API is used. Current [official outbound guidance](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-channel-outbound), [upstream dispatch cleanup](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/src/auto-reply/dispatch-dispatcher.ts) and inspected current upstream contracts do not establish an available replacement. A verified official extension would be preferable, but none is proven; a Benson-only hook/channel wrapper cannot compel every native branch. Do not postpone the decision for another undirected search or assume an upgrade fixes it.

**Recommended implementation, pending approval: one upstreamable native extension in existing owners, with Benson policy.** On `2026.10.4`, satisfying the full invariant requires a native/core change; it is not merely a hook failure-policy change or a replacement Dispatcher. Prefer that extension through official upstream support when available; the executable local alternative is an explicitly approved isolated core exception implementing the same contract. No second transport, queue, ledger, terminal manager or eligibility owner is introduced.

| Existing owner | Exact proposed responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Native admission/reply-operation/session and `ReplyCompletion` | Retain the authoritative reply obligation before accepted Run execution, original route/eligibility and stable native final-intent relationship. Converge success, tool-failure evidence, error, timeout, abort, cancellation and fallback through the native settlement owner before retiring a required reply. Use native reason/result data plus verified domain observations; missing effect evidence stays UNKNOWN. Model silence, tool/Run success or Run ID never settles eligibility. Stop ACK has its own requester/target binding. |
| Existing Benson Request/Response/contracts/presentation owners | Extend the already identified versioned contract evolution (§11.14) and host adapters, without treating current read admission or serialized classification as a Main grant. Native finalization submits typed terminal status, complete known/possible effects and candidate wording to the same Response Controller. It prepares the final or deterministic failure/UNKNOWN; no new Main Completion or verification model. If Controller authorization is unavailable, preserve an explicit recoverable failure in the existing native owner and send nothing; internal Controller reconstruction/retry remains Phase 2. |
| Native plugin/SDK integration | Provide one required, non-mutating outbound authorization provider through existing registration/lifecycle. Proposed operations are `authorize(finalCandidate, hostContext, abortSignal)` and `restore(storedBinding, recoveryContext, abortSignal)`; these are new proposed APIs, not existing exports. Host context derives from authoritative admission, not model/source labels. Missing/duplicate provider, unsupported context, exception, deadline or malformed decision blocks. Authorized decisions reference the existing eligibility owner and expose a synchronous currentness assertion; a serializable ID/hash alone is never a grant. |
| Native outbound prepare/plan/queue | Complete presentation rendering, channel normalization, trusted target resolution and ordered part planning before authorization. Freeze the exact semantic payload, route and native part plan. Native code computes/persists a versioned binding plus eligibility reference and stable intent in the existing prepared batch/queue; no new store. Adopt one final intent per reply obligation; identical replay adopts it, while changed payload/route is conflict. Native fallback/error candidates must not create another terminal intent. |
| Native adapter handoff and recovery | After every asynchronous preparation and immediately before I/O, enforce the bound plan and current authority alongside existing generation/claim/session-writer fences. Late authorization completion cannot revive a rejected attempt; post-authorization mutation rejects. **End-to-end invariant.** Let B=262144 and bytes(x)=Buffer.byteLength(JSON.stringify(x),'utf8'). DomainResult v3's inherited projection is {facts: complete normalized original facts, userResponse:content, completion:{gaps:evidence.gaps}}; all original data, warnings, domain failure, effects.details, uncertainty, pending context and gaps remain unchanged. This projection is a subset of a valid leaf Completion v5/v6 at the same JSON depth, with the existing trusted domain-identity normalization: removed routing/version/kind/results/outcome overhead exceeds the at-most12-byte null-to-bound-domain increase for a FAILED missing-domain record. Thus it remains <=B. The metadata projection keeps the actual v3 envelope with disjoint inherited variable leaves replaced by null/empty collections; refs, reconciliation, observations and handling remain separately bounded <=B. Restoring those leaves adds no more than the inherited bytes; validate the final <=2B envelope independently. No source Completion or values are duplicated on wire. Native Completion producers add only bounded refs/reconciliation with empty observations/null handling; even maximum64 UTF-8 identifiers fit this metadata budget. Response v4 validates/copies each result separately before aggregate traversal; {results:[]} metadata <=B. Its exact serialized size is metadata+sum(result bytes)+max(0,n-1), so the version-dependent cap in §4.4 covers all16 legal results without reducing their component depths. Actual text rendering remains separately bounded; no truncation or invented summary is permitted.

### 11.45 TS04 runtime-guidance producer/consumer alignment — approved 2026-11-02

**Observed pre-change incompatibility and owner.** Accepted baseline agents/jessica-vacuum/AGENTS.md Completion tells the child to copy its deterministic raw tool envelope. agents/reminder-service/AGENTS.md Result and completion contract requires the legacy status/domain/operation/verified/data/warnings/error/pendingContext object. The accepted typed owner `core/completion.ts:acceptAgentCompletion` instead requires exactly `schemaVersion:6`, `kind:domain-task`, `facts` and `userResponse`; host-only binding/results/completion are never model authority. The real TS03 fixture provider supplies this proposal explicitly, so its PASS does not establish compatibility with accepted baseline runtime instructions. Baseline main/AGENTS.md uses direct Task Entry only for status/list and delegates other requests through sessions_spawn/yield and legacy completion recovery/validation. This is an instruction-producer/typed-consumer mismatch, not an OpenClaw defect or permission grant.

**Exact regression and effects.** Extended the existing Completion v6 mutation-reporting regression with the exact raw tool JSON as final assistant content, retaining the production-shaped tool call/result/after-hook/terminal carrier. It proves that following the retained baseline reporting guidance yields controlled FAILED/unknown with a REPORT gap, while preserving verified effect details/refs and forbidding replay. The focused regression passed first, followed by all 10 tests in the affected Completion v6 suite. No production interaction was used. Existing reporting reconstruction remains valid defensive recovery; it cannot stand in for a positive NORMAL report producer.

**Approved focused change.** Oren explicitly approved this three-owner alignment and corresponding source/isolated integration. REPAIR main/AGENTS.md and the two domain AGENTS Completion/delegation sections to describe the already accepted Task Entry and v6 proposal, keeping all authorization/physical capability limits and canonical facts/effect owners. Main uses existing jessica_task/reminder_task with the reviewed current-source carriers, consumes complete validated DomainResult v2/v3 and passes final delivery through the common Response Controller. Child guidance returns only the four proposal fields above, preserves domain-owned verified facts/effects/uncertainty, and uses explicit unavailable wording when deterministic domain rendering is required. Runtime/domain adapters retain exact native correlation and build host-only fields; prompts do not confer authority. Retire contradictory legacy instructions only for the assembled typed release; preserve deployed code and rollback. No new tool, schema/version, store, service, permission, model activation or upstream modification is proposed. Source instruction changes do not authorize production loading.

**Alternatives and evidence.** Prefer aligning the instruction producer with the approved strict consumer over broadening acceptAgentCompletion to accept legacy raw envelopes as NORMAL, fabricating a positive report in host code, or shipping the fixture provider as product logic. Those alternatives would weaken evidence or create parallel semantics. Current [upstream hook documentation](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/docs/plugins/hooks.md) and [official hook catalog](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/hooks) distinguish native prompt/tool/lifecycle surfaces; installed 2026.10.4 types confirm api.on hooks and v2 invocation custody. None makes model JSON authority or proves this candidate's runtime composition. No new prompt hook or metadata channel is necessary merely to supply host-only binding fields: the accepted proposal deliberately omits them.

**Observed instruction acceptance and limits.** The approved three AGENTS owners now describe Task Entry delegation, the exact four-field v6 proposal and host-only native authority; physical limits, Calendar/recipient semantics, pending context and no-replay rules remain. Checkpoint originals/modes were verified before editing. Native `codex debug prompt-input` at main, Jessica and Reminder scopes was parsed in memory: every complete root and applicable nested AGENTS text occurred exactly once and ended with BENSON_AGENTS_EOF_V1. Only structured hashes/counts were retained in guidance-loading.json, never raw prompts. This proves local Codex instruction loading, not OpenClaw child bootstrap, model compliance or full flow acceptance. The exact legacy-report regression remains defensive failure coverage. Positive real-guidance Completion and assembled/recovery acceptance remain unproven; §11.46 owns the newly established capability blocker. §§11.6–11.8 acceptance is unchanged; no production exposure or Phase 2 deferral.

### 11.46 TS04 preserved-capability extension — approved 2026-11-02

**Invariant and exact evidence.** Preserving accepted discovery-to-mutation, clarification/history and complete user/delivery outcomes requires extending the deliberately bounded native Completion contract. §11.42 explicitly accepts a single-tool adapter and blocks unproven multi-tool compositions. core/completion.ts:NativeDomainCompletionInput has one tool/after pair; settleNativeDomainCompletion requires exactly one native call and result. The same probe proves NORMAL verified update/pause/resume are rejected by the existing domain renderer, and a create carrying pending secondary notification intents returns native_delivery_required. Response v3/v4 requires domain-rendered content before either deterministic or pass-through delivery; model prose cannot bypass that boundary. EXTEND the existing Completion/native adapters to validate one complete bounded ordered collection of actual native calls/results/after observations against terminal evidence, retaining unique identities, ordering, all known/possible effects, cancellation/lifetime custody and no replay. Preserve the current single-tool cases and strict v6/DomainResult v3/Response v4 wire validation; do not weaken final raw-evidence equality into accepting arbitrary semantic claims. No new tool, service, store, scheduler, schema owner, provider/model activation, SDK/core change, permission expansion or production exposure is included. **Research and alternatives.** Current [upstream plugin hooks](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/docs/plugins/hooks.md) and [official background runtime documentation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/sdk-runtime/background-work) support typed per-tool observations, terminal messages and native run/wait custody; wait timeout is not cancellation or terminal proof. **Semantic pending-resolution boundary — bounded facts extension approved 2026-11-02.** A fresh read-only producer audit establishes that complete original Main Task results do not generally identify which earlier clarification a terminal result consumed. Reminder adds its existing contextId, revision and currentEvidenceId: contextId/revision alone can collide across requests in one conversation. Jessica adds its existing clarificationId, with no invented revision. Outer Completion v6, DomainResult v2/v3, Response v4 and raw domain versions remain unchanged. No new store, tool, orchestration, Core patch or transaction-record migration is authorized.

Exact terminal success or domain-verified terminal failure may close only the selected pending; clarification follow-up replaces only that pending with a fresh domain identity/revision. Reused/conflicting/foreign identities reject. Expired pending cannot be selected, completed input cannot replay, and source retirement invalidates selection; retaining read custody across own native appends grants no execution. Reload reconstructs from existing result carriers, with no second store. This source slice does not establish native continuation, cancellation/abort settlement or concurrency admission. Those remain mandatory TS04 acceptance, alongside exact pre-effect selection and original custody across all producer paths. Native blocked/failed/synthetic/missing/unsealed receipts must never become closure evidence. Product producers remain disabled until the complete path passes isolated integration and acceptance; no Phase1 guarantee is deferred.

**New verified integration gate — explicit pre-effect selection.** Source inspection and the retained pending-selection-gap.json counterexample establish a separate input-side gap in Jessica: TaskEnvelope v1 has only kind/version/request; ExecuteRequest has operation/target/settings (or a control operation); neither identifies an earlier clarification. Two distinct pending requests with overlapping candidates can have the same authenticated current answer, valid execution arguments and domain outcome. A host callback injected in a test, room/operation matching, a latest/unique heuristic, child session identity, or a model report after dispatch cannot establish which original pending was selected before effects. The existing executor remains the authority for policy, Operation State, dispatch and outcome; it cannot manufacture the missing semantic choice. The installed runtime exposes launch/wait/transcript access, not this domain choice; current official runtime documentation distinguishes transcript access from execution authority. The approved result-side reference solves historical representation, not this input omission. No additional Core patch is demonstrated necessary.

**Approved minimal input extension — 2026-11-02.** Oren approved TaskEnvelope v2 with exact current request and required pending:null|ConsumedPending; §11.50 records implementation and evidence. Main proposes the exact reference; the domain host validates complete authorized original Task history, binding/revision/expiry, current input and domain applicability before child launch and again before dispatch. Backend arguments, Operation State and authorization remain unchanged. Strict v1 stays current-only; null never auto-selects. Basic unambiguous continuation is prioritized; ambiguous or unsupported multi-pending cases fail closed. No new tool/store/Core/orchestration or broader state/lifecycle design is approved. Full acceptance still requires actual separately loaded domain/Response flows, replacements, reload/recovery, concurrent/duplicate inputs, cancellation, terminal failure versus uncertainty, unchanged effects and no replay. Product pending-dependent effects remain disabled until that acceptance. Typed Jessica entry, broader effects/presentation, resource/recovery closure and stable-candidate review remain open.

**Checkpoint and rollback.** Reuse .checkpoints/ts04-assembly-20261101; pending-before.json records the exact prior candidate bytes/modes for affected existing paths and the absence of the planned Jessica entry. All previous checkpoints, outstanding AGENTS changes, deployed dist, unrelated DREAMS and both approved patch inventories remain protected. The existing guarded rollback.mjs remains the sole source rollback mechanism; its --check must pass against the updated candidate inventory before --apply. Slice-only recovery uses the exact pending-before manifest/copies, with current-candidate preflight and post-restore hash/mode comparison; no source restore can undo domain/external effects. This slice has none. Contract validation evidence and precise limitations are indexed in pending-contract-validation.json; complete TS04 acceptance and independent stable-candidate review are not claimed.

**Observed source validation — 2026-11-02.** Strict build and 190/190 affected source/fixture tests PASS across eight suites. These include eight new context cases and two carrier/presentation cases: exact selection among five pending sharing conversation/revision, clarification replacement, Jessica identities without fabricated revisions, JSON reload, legacy null non-clearing, terminal failure, foreign/stale/missing/duplicate references, expiry/source retirement, completed-input rejection, failed reporting/unresolved-effect rejection, getter preflight, full-size source data and unchanged domain presentation through v3/v4. Stable integrated acceptance and its configured independent review await the explicit pre-effect selection decision and remaining TS04 work.


**Limits and comparison.** Hooks alone lack commit/branch completeness. Hooks plus the supported readers are the preferred candidate and have not been disproved. The synchronous reader limits lazy payload hydration but scans native navigation metadata before its callback; a generally bounded native scan is not proven, and existing P03 budgets must not be weakened. RawDelta metadata uses maxEvents + 1 before payload hydration, but preflight and synchronous context snapshots can race with peer appends; the supported write lock is process-local. Public readers may also perform cold restore/index maintenance outside page budgets. These verified limits leave measured TS04 resource closure open; they neither prove another Core patch necessary nor move the G10 production fast-path activation gate into TS04. Compaction/reset/excludeFromContext may omit originals; omission is unavailable evidence, never proof of no pending. Extending the existing WhatsApp Plugin handoff remains an option only if actual supported evidence cannot reach the current adapter; no second store, orchestration or lifecycle owner is authorized. An additional Core exposure may be proposed only after a smallest required-flow regression demonstrates an unavoidable missing guarantee and alternatives are exhausted, with explicit approval before implementation. The separately approved §11.48 receipt/seal exception remains independent and preserved.

**Research and recovery.** Current [official persistence-hook documentation](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/hooks/tool-policy), [session schema](https://docs.openclaw.ai/reference/session-management-compaction/schema) and [upstream session manager](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/blob/main/src/agents/sessions/session-manager.ts) inform the comparison; installed bytes/native proof establish this pin's behavior, not rolling documentation.
